Top-5 balance under 250: the non-figure slice gains four (aapl-09, msft-01, msft-07, msft-08) and loses two (msft-05, nvda-01), 21 to 23 of 30; the figure slice loses three (msft-12, nvda-11, nvda-14), 12 to 9 of 12; AAPL gains one (13 to 14), MSFT gains three and loses two (11 to 12), NVDA loses three (9 to 6). Aggregate 33 to 32. Rows 248 and 249 differ from 250 as listed in the rule bullets and `rule-table.txt` (for example msft-04 falls out of the top 5 at 10 and 20 candidates but rises to 1 at 40, and msft-08 is still no match at 10).
        * The five long-standing misses: nvda-02, nvda-04, and nvda-09 are no match in all four rows; nvda-07 moves from no match to 6 (20 candidates) and 7 (40), still outside the top 5; msft-08 moves from no match to 2 (chunk 547) at 20 and 40 candidates and stays no match at 10. For nvda-02 the expected passage is only in chunk 802, which is not among the first 20 fused candidates (`POST /api/rag/retrieve` with topK 20 and `"rerank": false`), so at 10 or 20 candidates the reranker never sees it, and at 40 it is not in the reranked top 10; whether it lies in fused positions 21 to 40 was not determined (2026-09-14: this holds for the head rows, which carry no trace; the traced windowed runs record chunk 802's fused position for nvda-02 in the generated blocks of Windowed rows below). The plan's premise that a neighbouring chunk merely outranks the answer does not hold for nvda-02 at 10 or 20 candidates under the current fusion (at 40 it was not determined). The same chunk 802 answers nvda-12, which reranking lifted from 5 to 2 or 3.
        * Retrieval check for nvda-02 (cross-encoder enabled, `rerank-candidates` 20): body `{"ticker":"NVDA","query":"By what percentage did NVIDIA's Data Center revenue grow in fiscal 2026?","topK":10,"latestFilingsOnly":true,"rerank":false}` returns HYBRID_RRF, chunks 806, 852, 805, 851, 881, 873, 745, 876, 800, 744, expected passage not present (no match, as in 247); with `"rerank": true` HYBRID_RRF_RERANKED, chunks 852, 873, 881, 863, 880, 806, 800, 876, 745, 842, expected passage not present (no match, as in 249), identical on a second call.
        * Recommendation: do not enable reranking. `rag.retrieval.reranking-enabled` and `rag.retrieval.cross-encoder.enabled` stay false; `rag.retrieval.rerank-candidates` stays 20 (no row won, so the table gives no reason to move it). Neither floor is re-derived: no row qualified, and with reranking off the floors still guard snapshot 91's ranks (live test exit 0, below).
        * What enabling would take if Jay chose to anyway (against this measurement): `rag.retrieval.cross-encoder.enabled=true` (`RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_ENABLED=true`) and `rag.retrieval.reranking-enabled=true`, optionally `rag.retrieval.rerank-candidates=40` (the best MRR of the three, snapshot 250). Startup then fails on any machine without `models/cross-encoder-ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2/model.onnx` and `tokenizer.json` matching the recorded checksums, because the files are not in git. Each retrieval call would add about 0.26 s, 0.52 s, or 0.98 s of scoring at 10, 20, or 40 candidates (topK 10), and the first scoring call after a start took 446 to 1,110 ms (the warm-up calls, topK 5); at 40 candidates the slowest call (1,103 ms) is about half the 2,000 ms `rerank-timeout-ms`. Expect what the table shows at 40 candidates, gains and losses together: aggregate hit@5 0.786 to 0.762, NVDA hit@5 0.643 to 0.429, and the figure slice in the top 5 12/12 to 9/12, against a net non-figure change of plus two (21/30 to 23/30) and AAPL 13/14 to 14/14, from a single run.
        * Floors with the final configuration (defaults unchanged, reranking off): `set -a && source .env && set +a && ./mvnw -q -o test -Dtest=RetrievalEvaluationLiveTests -Drag.evaluation.live=true` exit 0, hit@5 0.785714 (floor 0.65, 33 of 42, margin 5), non-figure 21 of 30 (floor 0.60, margin 3); `retrieval_evaluations` 26 rows, max id 250, before and after (rolled back).

Every rank, metric, rule outcome, candidate position, token span, and row of the windowed and traced rows stated below is a block bullet; the prose bullets of this subsection state how the runs were executed and reproduced, scoring time and load, the floor tests, decisions, and where the evidence lies, each naming its file, and cite no claim.
            * What was run (`live-runs/2026-09-13-reranker-windows/measurement/run.log`): set v2 (42 questions), window 10, the fusion defaults, one application start per row with `RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_ENABLED=true` and the row's overrides as environment variables of the run shell only, one warm-up `POST /api/rag/retrieve` with `"rerank": true` after each start, then `POST /api/rag/evaluate?rerank=false` (reference 295) or `?rerank=true`: 296, 297, and 298 with `RAG_RETRIEVAL_RERANK_CANDIDATES` 10, 20, and 40 (`max-window/overlap=64/maxWindows=4`), and 299 with 20 and `RAG_RETRIEVAL_CROSS_ENCODER_WINDOW_OVERLAP_TOKENS=224` (`max-window/overlap=224/maxWindows=4`). Row 298 ran with `RAG_RETRIEVAL_RERANK_TIMEOUT_MS=4000` from its start (plan Amendment 1), a setting snapshots do not record. The run log's harness says each start's `scoring=` line confirms the overlap in effect, and the log quotes that line for run 5 only (the row with overlap 224); it lists each snapshot's reranking `properties`, records `rerankFallbackQuestions` 0 for the four rerank rows, and records that no row was rerun (corrected 2026-09-14, Milestone 4b remediation round 1: this sentence said the run log quotes each start's `scoring=` line). No chat model; 267 embeddings in all.
            * Traced runs (`live-runs/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence/measurement/run.log`, Milestone 4a): one application start per row on port 8082 with the same warm-up and `trace=true`; snapshots 598, 599, 613, 627, and 641 ran at the settings of 295, 296, 297, 298, and 299, in that order. The run log records the one-minute load average when each evaluate call started (3.03, 3.75, 3.69, 6.49, and 7.57) and `rerankFallbackQuestions` 0 for the four traced rerank rows. Run 627's `RAG_RETRIEVAL_RERANK_TIMEOUT_MS=4000` is a run record: its committed files show only that its calls ran up to 3,072 ms without fallback: the slowest of its 42 evaluation calls took 3,072 ms (`scoring-627.txt`), and the run log records no fallback for the run (corrected 2026-09-14, Milestone 4b remediation round 1: this sentence said the files show that the timeout exceeded 3,072 ms). `TraceReproductionCheck` compared each traced row with its untraced counterpart (rank and matched chunk of the 42 questions, fallbacks, errors, and the counterpart's recorded properties) and wrote problems=0 for the five pairs (`reproduction-598.txt` to `reproduction-641.txt`).
            * Scoring time (`live-runs/2026-09-13-reranker-windows/measurement/latency-296.txt` to `latency-299.txt`: the `Cross-encoder scoring completed ... windows=..., elapsedMs=` lines of the 42 evaluation calls of each rerank row, topK 10, warm-up excluded; windows is the rows scored in the call): median and maximum 539.5 and 623 ms with a median of 21 windows per call (296), 1,122 and 1,228 ms with 43 (297), 2,139 and 2,347 ms with 83.5 (298), 1,323 and 1,612 ms with 51 (299). In 298, 27 of the 42 evaluation calls took over 2,000 ms, and so did its warm-up call (2,403 ms). The traced rows' calls (`scoring-599.txt` to `scoring-641.txt`, the same lines) have medians of 586, 1,181, 2,342.5, and 1,409.5 ms and maxima of 1,508, 1,840, 3,072, and 1,730 ms.
            * Reading the blocks: rank, top-k, metric, and rule bullets read the untraced snapshot files 295 to 299 (and the head rows 248 to 250 where they are listed), except where a bullet names a traced snapshot; candidate, token-span, and row bullets read the traced rows' evidence reports (`evidence-598.json` to `evidence-641.json`). A row is called scored for a chunk only when the chunk was a rerank input of a reranked question and the trace's window count equals the rows of the arithmetic (Retrieval Evaluation, Claims, Sentences). No check type renders a matched chunk id, a score, a window start, or the chunks ranked above an accepted chunk, so the blocks state reranked positions instead: matched chunk ids are the snapshots' `matchedChunkId`; scores, per-window scores, and the chunks ranked above each question's best accepted chunk are the `score`, `windowScores`, and `rankedAbove` values of each evidence report, and the measurement `run.log` (section Milestone 4b) lists the `rankedAbove` chunk ids for nvda-01, nvda-11, and msft-04 as copied from the reports.

* RetrievalRequest
    * Required fields
        * ticker: nonblank, maximum 16 characters.
        * query: nonblank, maximum 4000 characters.
    * Optional fields
        * filingTypes: nonempty list when supplied; for example ["10-K", "10-Q"].
        * filingDateFrom: inclusive lower filing-date bound.
        * filingDateTo: inclusive upper filing-date bound.
        * sectionKeys: nonempty list when supplied; for example ["ITEM_1A"].
        * topK: final result count, between 1 and 20; default 5.
        * latestFilingsOnly: explicit override for latest-per-type selection.
        * hybrid: true forces keyword plus vector fusion for this call, false forces vector only; absent follows `rag.retrieval.hybrid-enabled`.
        * rerank: true reranks this call (HTTP 400 when no FilingReranker is configured), false keeps the fused order; absent follows `rag.retrieval.reranking-enabled`.
    * Default filing scope
        * Without dates, use the latest stored EMBEDDED filing per type by default.
        * With either date bound, search all eligible filings in that range by default.
        * An explicit latestFilingsOnly value overrides either default.
        * When enabled with dates, latest means latest within the supplied date range.
        * Omitted filingTypes means all stored filing types, with the same latest-per-type rule.
        * Latest is ordered by filingDate, then accessionNo and database ID for ties.
        * Latest selection occurs before the section filter; it does not fall back to older filings if a section is absent.
    * Date semantics
        * Bounds apply to filingDate, not reportDate.
        * This date-only baseline does not guarantee intraday historical availability.
* FilingRetrievalFilter
    * Purpose
        * Carry normalized company, filing type, date, section, and latest-filings constraints into SQL.
* RetrievedFilingChunk
    * Purpose
        * Represent one retrieved SEC evidence passage and its provenance.
    * Fields
        * chunkId
        * filingId
        * ticker
        * cik
        * accessionNo
        * filingType
        * filingDate
        * reportDate
        * sectionKey
        * sectionTitle
        * chunkIndex
        * content
        * sourceUrl
        * similarityScore
* RetrievalResponse
    * Fields
        * ticker: normalized company ticker.
        * query: trimmed query.
        * retrievalStrategy: FILTERED_VECTOR or FILTERED_VECTOR_RERANKED; HYBRID_RRF or HYBRID_RRF_RERANKED when the keyword search ran and returned (an empty keyword result included), FILTERED_VECTOR when hybrid is off, the query has no keyword terms, or the keyword search failed.
        * latestFilingsOnly: resolved filing-selection policy.
        * topK: requested/default final result limit.
        * candidatesRetrieved: candidate count before final selection.
        * results: evidence passages with citations.
    * Empty results
        * Return HTTP 200 with an empty results list when no eligible chunks match the scope.
        * Retrieval searches stored filings and does not fetch missing companies from SEC.
        * Empty results do not trigger an automatic ingestion or answer-generation call.
    * Baseline limitations
        * Results are nearest passages, not a guarantee the query is answerable.
        * Overlapping chunks may still appear together; contextual deduplication is deferred.
        * Model reranking exists but is off by default (Cross-encoder reranker; its 2026-09-13 measurements under head and windowed scoring, with their selection-rule outcomes, are in Reranker measurement there; reworded 2026-09-14, plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md` Milestone 4b remediation round 2: this parenthesis restated the rule outcome, readable at commit 43b1931) and answer generation remains a separate next step; hybrid keyword retrieval is in place with tuned weights and a figure leg (Hybrid Retrieval and Fusion tuning below, RAG-12 done).

* Ingestion Pipeline
  Ticker
  ↓
  SECClient
  ↓
  Raw SEC HTML
  ↓
  FilingHtmlParser
  ↓
  List
  ↓
  FilingChunker
  ↓
  List
  ↓
  FilingIngestionService
  ↓
  FilingChunk entities
  ↓
  FilingEmbeddingService
  ↓
  Embedding vectors
  ↓
  PostgreSQL + pgvector


* Retrieval Methods (overview, as of 2026-09-13)
    * What retrieval is for
        * Given a ticker and a question, return the few stored filing passages most likely to hold the answer. The recommendation loop searches with the user's question before the RAG specialist model runs and again on the specialist's own queries; the passages returned become the evidence the manager reasons over and cites, and the critic checks the answer against them. Retrieval quality therefore bounds recommendation quality.
    * The eligibility pool (one SQL CTE shared by every method)
        * Only the requested ticker; only filings whose ingestion status is EMBEDDED; by default only the latest stored filing of each type (10-K, 10-Q, 8-K), unless a date range is given; optional filing-type and section filters. Every method below draws candidates from exactly this pool, so combining them never widens what is searched.
    * Method 1: vector similarity (since 2026-09-09)
        * The question is embedded once with `text-embedding-3-small` (1536 dimensions). Each chunk's stored embedding is compared by cosine distance in pgvector (`<=>`), exact search over the pool, `candidate-count` 40 nearest chunks. Strong on paraphrase and narrative questions ("why did margins fall"), weak on exact tokens: an embedding blurs "215,938" or "OpenAI" into their surroundings.
    * Method 2: keyword search (since 2026-09-12, RAG-2)
        * PostgreSQL full-text search over a stored generated column `content_tsv = to_tsvector('english', content)` with a GIN index (migration V9). The question is turned into an OR query of its distinct alphanumeric tokens (stopwords dropped; numbers keep their inner commas and periods, so "64,377" matches only the phrase '64' followed by '377'), bound as a parameter to `to_tsquery`, never concatenated. Ranked by `ts_rank_cd`, `keyword-candidate-count` 40. Finds exact terms and figures; scores common words as much as rare ones, which is why it is fused rather than used alone.
    * Method 3: figure search (since 2026-09-12, RAG-12)
        * Runs only when the question carries a number that is not a lone year: an AND query of the question's numeric tokens over the same column, so a chunk must contain every number. Rewards the single chunk that states the figure ("Revenue $ 215,938") over neighbours that share the surrounding words. Off for any question without a non-year number, whatever its kind.
    * Combining them: weighted reciprocal rank fusion
        * Each method yields a ranked list. A chunk's fused score is the sum, over the lists containing it, of weight / (k + rank), with k = 60 and weights vector 1.0, keyword 0.5, figure 1.0 (chosen by measurement, see Fusion tuning). Rank-based fusion needs no calibration between cosine similarity and text-search rank; the weights and k are configuration. Ties break by vector similarity, then chunk id. Exact decimal arithmetic makes the order deterministic.
        * Every returned chunk still reports its cosine similarity to the question, whichever method found it, so the recommendation loop's input-coverage confidence keeps its meaning. If the keyword method fails, retrieval degrades to vector-only and says so in `retrievalStrategy` (FILTERED_VECTOR instead of HYBRID_RRF); it never fails because of the keyword path.
    * After fusion
        * Diversify: near-duplicate chunks from the same filing and section (large text overlap) are dropped so the top-k is not five copies of one passage. Then the requested top-k (5 by default; 3 under the lean profile) is returned with citation metadata. A reranker hook (`FilingReranker`) can re-order the fused, diversified candidates with a model: the local cross-encoder (Cross-encoder reranker above) when `rag.retrieval.cross-encoder.enabled` is true and a call asks for it; reranking is off by default, and the 2026-09-13 measurements under head scoring and then windowed scoring, with their selection-rule outcomes, are in Cross-encoder reranker, Reranker measurement (RAG-1; reworded 2026-09-14, plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md` Milestone 4b remediation round 2: this sentence restated the rule outcome, readable at commit 43b1931).
    * Per-request control
        * `hybrid` on `POST /api/rag/retrieve` and `?hybrid=` on `POST /api/rag/evaluate` override the property default for one call, which is how the two strategies are compared without a restart.
    * How it is measured
        * The evaluation sets (Retrieval Evaluation below; v1 has 30 questions, v2, the default since 2026-09-13, has 42) record hit@1/3/5 and MRR per stored snapshot. Vector-only: hit@5 0.600, MRR 0.436 (snapshot 35). Hybrid, equal weights: 0.633, 0.437 (snapshot 34). Hybrid with the tuned weights: 0.633, MRR 0.463, hit@1 0.333 (snapshot 51). Set v1 carries no figure-bearing questions, so under v1 the figure method was evidenced only by live queries. Set v2 (42 questions, the default since 2026-09-13, RAG-11) adds 12 figure-bearing questions and makes the figure method measurable: the current default configuration is still snapshot 51's weights, now also confirmed on set v2 (snapshot 69: hit@5 0.786, MRR 0.655), and turning the figure method off drops v2 hit@5 to 0.738 and MRR from 0.655 to 0.533 (snapshot 71). The v2 numbers are not comparable with v1's and are not a retrieval improvement over 0.633: the 12 new questions state their figure in their own text and 12 of 12 are in the top 5 under snapshot 69 (reworded 2026-09-14, Milestone 4b remediation round 2: "all hit"), while the 30 carried questions score 21 of 30 (see Set v2 baseline and figure-leg measurement below). NVDA is still the weakest ticker (v2 hit@5 0.643 under snapshot 69; 0.3 on v1) for structural reasons recorded in RAG-7 and RAG-1. The cross-encoder reranker was measured on set v2 under head scoring at 10, 20, and 40 candidates (snapshots 248 to 250 against reference 247, one run per configuration); its metrics, per-question changes, and selection-rule outcomes are stated in Cross-encoder reranker, Reranker measurement (reworded 2026-09-14, plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md` Milestone 4b remediation round 2: this sentence restated the head rows' metrics with causal wording and the reference's reproduction of snapshot 91; it can be read at commit 43b1931). Windowed scoring was measured on set v2 at the same candidate counts and at 20 candidates with a 224-token overlap (snapshots 296 to 299 against reference 295, one run per configuration); its metrics, per-question ranks, and selection-rule outcomes are stated in the generated blocks of Cross-encoder reranker, Reranker measurement, Windowed rows, and reranking is not enabled (replaced 2026-09-14, plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md` Milestone 4b remediation round 1: the hand-written summary of the windowed rows that stood here used causal wording and stated that reference 295 reproduces 247, which the generated blocks do not state; it can be read at commit 1832b4a).
    * What is deliberately not done
        * No approximate vector index (exact search over a few hundred chunks per ticker is fast); no reranking by default (the cross-encoder exists and was measured on 2026-09-13 on set v2, one run per configuration, under head scoring, snapshots 248 to 250, and under windowed scoring, snapshots 296 to 299; the measured values and selection-rule outcomes are stated in Cross-encoder reranker, Reranker measurement, the windowed ones in the generated blocks of its Windowed rows subsection, whose Recommendation keeps reranking off; RAG-1; reworded 2026-09-14, plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md` Milestone 4b remediation round 2: this parenthesis restated both measurements' metrics, the questions leaving the top 5, and the rule outcome, with causal wording for the head rows; the previous text can be read at commit 43b1931); no query rewriting or expansion; no cross-ticker search; passages are cut only at the model boundary (`recommendation.model-passage-chars`), never in the store.

* Retrieval Pipeline
  User Query + Ticker + Optional Filters
  ↓
  FilingRetrievalController
  ↓
  FilingRetrievalService
  ↓
  FilingEmbeddingService.embed(query)
  ↓
  FilingRetrievalRepository
  ↓
  Filter Eligible Filings and Chunks (one CTE shared by both legs)
  ↓
  Exact pgvector Cosine Similarity Search ∥ Full-text Keyword Search (content_tsv, GIN; hybrid-enabled, default true) ∥ Figure Search (AND of the query's numbers; only when the query carries one)
  ↓
  Weighted Reciprocal Rank Fusion (k = 60; weights vector 1.0, keyword 0.5, figure 1.0), then Diversify
  ↓
  Top Candidate Filing Chunks
  ↓
  Optional FilingReranker (local cross-encoder; bean off by default, reranking off by default)
  ↓
  Top-K SEC Evidence + Citations
  ↓
  RetrievalResponse
  ↓
  Optional Recommendation Pipeline (see Agent_Harness.md)

* Retrieval Request Example
    * Latest stored AAPL 10-K, top 5 passages.

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/rag/retrieve \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{
    "ticker": "AAPL",
    "query": "What are the main risks to operating margins?",
    "filingTypes": ["10-K"],
    "topK": 5
  }'
```

* Historical Retrieval Example
    * Search all stored AAPL 10-K/10-Q filings published during the specified date range.

```json
{
  "ticker": "AAPL",
  "query": "What risks affected profitability?",
  "filingTypes": ["10-K", "10-Q"],
  "filingDateFrom": "2024-01-01",
  "filingDateTo": "2025-12-31",
  "latestFilingsOnly": false,
  "topK": 5
}
```

* Retrieval Design Decisions
    * See [Retrieval Strategy Research](Retrieval_Strategy_Research.md) for source research and alternatives.
    * Current baseline: filtered exact vector search fused with full-text keyword search by weighted reciprocal rank (since 2026-09-12; Hybrid Retrieval below), the keyword leg at 0.5 and a figure leg at 1.0 for queries that carry a number (Fusion tuning below; the figure weight re-measured and kept on set v2, Set v2 baseline and figure-leg measurement below).
    * Reranker provider/model and default filing policy were raised for user input.
    * In the absence of a different choice, use the recommended configurable defaults above.

* Retrieval Verification
    * 22 new regression checks cover request validation, service selection, optional reranker contracts, and database retrieval.
    * PostgreSQL tests use known vectors to verify cosine ranking, inclusive date filters, filing types, sections, and latest-per-type selection.
    * Null/zero stored embeddings and failed filings are excluded.
    * The full 47-test suite passed against a disposable PostgreSQL/pgvector database.
    * Correctness tests do not establish real-world retrieval relevance or reranker quality.

* Retrieval Evaluation
    * Purpose
        * A fixed, versioned question set with known-good passages, measured the same way every time, so a retrieval or prompt change (reranking RAG-1, hybrid retrieval RAG-2, lean-profile passage tuning AGENT-9, prompt regression AGENT-8) is judged against a baseline instead of a single live run. Closes Follow_Ups AGENT-3.
    * The set
        * `src/main/resources/evaluation/retrieval-set-v1.json`: 30 analyst-style questions (10 each for AAPL, MSFT, NVDA) written against the latest stored 10-K, 10-Q, and 8-K per ticker as of 2026-09-12.
        * `src/main/resources/evaluation/retrieval-set-v2.json` (Follow_Ups RAG-11; plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-set-v2.md`, Milestone 1): the default since 2026-09-13. It carries the 30 v1 questions unchanged in id, ticker, kind, and question text, with alternative expectations where another stored passage genuinely answers the question (nvda-01 the adjacent segment table, nvda-03 Item 5 and the equity note, and aapl-01, aapl-02, aapl-05, aapl-06, msft-01, msft-02, msft-05, msft-07, msft-10, nvda-06 where a note, MD&A paragraph, or the latest 10-Q restates the fact), plus 12 new FIGURE questions (aapl-11 to aapl-14, msft-11 to msft-14, nvda-11 to nvda-14) whose text states the figure in the analyst's own words rather than the filing sentence's, so `FilingRetrievalRepository.figureTerms` is non-empty and the figure leg runs for them; each new question's figure tokens are all present in its expected chunk. A passage counts as an alternative only if it answers the question completely on its own, every part of a multi-part question included (plan Amendment 1): msft-07's alternative is the 10-Q Item 1A passage with the goals and why AI makes them harder, not the 10-K Item 1 paragraph that states the goals alone, and nvda-07 keeps its single 10-K Item 1A expectation because only that chunk states the manufacturing and final-assembly concentration, so a miss on nvda-07 is a genuine retrieval miss. 42 questions, 14 per ticker.
        * Selecting a set: `rag.evaluation.set` names the classpath resource (default `evaluation/retrieval-set-v2.json`; blank is rejected at startup and a resource that is not on the classpath fails the load with an IllegalStateException naming it). Set v1 stays bundled unchanged, so `rag.evaluation.set=evaluation/retrieval-set-v1.json` reproduces any pre-v2 snapshot. Every snapshot records the resource as `properties.set` beside `setVersion`.
        * Metrics are not comparable across sets: a v2 number can be higher or lower than a v1 number purely because the questions and expectations differ. Compare snapshots only when their `setVersion` matches; pre-v2 snapshots (up to and including the fusion tuning) are comparable only with each other.
        * Format: `version`, `createdOn`, and `questions`; each question has `id`, `ticker`, `kind` (FIGURE for an exact number stated in the filing, NARRATIVE for a risk, segment change, or policy), `question`, `expected` (one or more passages, any one satisfies), and optional `notes`.
        * An expected passage is `accessionNo`, `sectionKey`, and `phrase`: a verbatim 12 to 200 character excerpt of a chunk stored for that filing and section, using the same characters as the chunk (curly quotes, non-breaking spaces). Chunk IDs change on rebuild, so they are never referenced.
        * `RetrievalEvaluationSetLoader` (`rag.evaluation`) reads the resource selected by `rag.evaluation.set` (`load()`, or `load(resource)` for an explicit one) and rejects duplicate ids, empty expectation lists, blank or out-of-range phrases, malformed tickers or accession numbers, and phrases shared by two questions, naming the question id.
        * `RetrievalEvaluationSetTests` (database-backed, read-only) proves every expectation is a substring of a stored chunk, case-insensitive with whitespace collapsed, once for the set `rag.evaluation.set` selects and once explicitly for v1, naming the question id and expectation on a miss. `RetrievalEvaluationSetLoaderTests` asserts the v2 invariants without the database (v1 questions carried unchanged, at least 10 questions with figure terms and 3 per ticker, alternatives on nvda-01 and nvda-03, a single expectation on nvda-07, and no run of five or more words shared between msft-11, msft-12, msft-14, or nvda-13 and its phrase). Section keys follow the parser: NVIDIA's Item 8 is a one-line cross-reference, so its financial statement notes sit under ITEM_15; the AAPL 8-K Item 2.02 is stored as ITEM_2 and the MSFT 8-K Item 7.01 as ITEM_7_01.
        * Nothing in the set reaches a model prompt; an evaluation run embeds each question once (the only external call) and never calls a chat model.
    * Adding a question
        * Find the chunk that answers it: `SELECT c.id, c.section_key, c.content FROM sec_filing_chunks c JOIN sec_filings f ON f.id = c.filing_id WHERE f.accession_no = '<accession>' AND c.content ILIKE '%<distinctive words>%'`, then copy a 12 to 200 character excerpt exactly as stored (the loader does not fix quotes or spaces).
        * Add the question with the next id for its ticker (`nvda-11`), the accession, the section key as stored, and the phrase; add a second expected passage when the same fact is stated in another section or chunk, so a correct retrieval of either counts.
        * Keep the set invariants: v1 30 questions and v2 40 to 44, at least 8 per ticker, at least 6 FIGURE questions (v2: at least 10 questions whose text carries a figure, 3 per ticker), no phrase used twice, sections ITEM_1A, ITEM_7, ITEM_8, ITEM_1, and ITEM_7_01 all covered. For a figure question, state the figure in the question and avoid numbers the chunk does not contain (a year or a "31" from a date rides along in the figure leg's AND); v1 is frozen, so add questions to v2 or a new version.
        * Run `RetrievalEvaluationSetTests` (proves the phrase is stored) and then a live evaluation; when the set changes in a way that moves the metrics, bump `version`, record a new baseline below, and re-derive the floor. Stored snapshots carry `setVersion`, so old ones stay comparable among themselves.
    * Metrics (`RetrievalEvaluationService`)
        * Each question is retrieved once with its ticker, the question text as the query, `latestFilingsOnly` true, and `topK` = window; no section, filing type, or date filter, so the section filter never helps the evaluation.
        * Rank: the 1-based position of the first returned chunk whose accession and section equal an expected passage's and whose content contains the phrase (case-insensitive, whitespace collapsed); null when no chunk in the window matches.
        * hit@k: the fraction of questions with rank at most k; reported at k = 1, 3, 5.
        * MRR: the mean over all questions of 1/rank, counting a null rank as 0; a question found at rank 10 contributes 0.1, so MRR rewards moving a passage up even when hit@5 does not change.
        * Per-ticker hit@5: hit@5 over each ticker's questions, the first place to look when a change helps one filer and hurts another.
        * Window: `rag.evaluation.window` (default 10, 5 to 20); a passage beyond it is a miss, so the window bounds MRR's tail and the cost of a run (one embedding per question regardless of window).
        * A retrieval exception for one question is recorded as a miss with the error string; the other questions still count, so one embedding failure never voids a run.
        * Slices (since 2026-09-13): each question is also counted in exactly one slice by its text, with the rule that decides whether retrieval's figure leg runs: `figure` when `FilingRetrievalRepository.figureTerms(question)` is non-empty (a numeric token that is not a lone year, such as "$64,377 million" or "40%"), `nonFigure` otherwise ("revenue in fiscal 2025" is non-figure). A numeric token is a standalone run of digits (inner commas or periods allowed); digits inside a word are not one, so nvda-10's "H200" makes it a non-figure question. Reading "H200" as a figure would give a 13 / 29 split instead of the stored 12 / 30. Each slice records `questionCount`, `hitAt1`, `hitAt3`, `hitAt5`, `mrr` (same definitions and scale as the aggregate, computed by the same code over the slice's results) and `missIds` (the slice's questions with no match in the window, a retrieval error included, in set order; a question ranked 6 to 10 is not a miss but is not a hit@5 either) and `notInTop5` (every question of the slice that does not count toward its hit@5, rank null or greater than 5, in set order, each as `{id, rank}` with rank null for no match in the window or an error; so the ranked 6 to 10 questions are listed with their ranks). Snapshots stored before `notInTop5` was added (snapshot 91 and earlier) read back with it null. An empty slice has `questionCount` 0 and null metrics. The rule, not the set version, defines the slices, so they carry to any future set; on v2 they are exactly the 12 questions added in v2 and the 30 carried from v1.
    * RetrievalEvaluationProperties
        * Configuration prefix: rag.evaluation.

| Property | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| rag.evaluation.set | evaluation/retrieval-set-v2.json | Classpath resource of the set every run evaluates, recorded as `properties.set`; `evaluation/retrieval-set-v1.json` for comparison runs; must not be blank |
| rag.evaluation.window | 10 | Chunks retrieved per question, 5 to 20; a passage beyond it is a miss |
| rag.evaluation.min-hit-at-5 | 0.65 | Floor asserted by the opt-in `RetrievalEvaluationLiveTests`; derived on set v2 (snapshot 69), superseding the set v1 floor of 0.50 |
| rag.evaluation.min-non-figure-hit-at-5 | 0.60 | Second floor, on the `nonFigure` slice's hit@5, asserted by the same test after the aggregate floor; derived on set v2 (snapshot 91, 21 of 30); 0 to 1; needs 18 of 30 |

    * Endpoints (integration token required, `Authorization: Bearer <INTEGRATION_ACCESS_TOKEN>`)
        * `POST /api/rag/evaluate` runs every question through retrieval and stores a snapshot in `retrieval_evaluations` (migration V8); `GET /api/rag/evaluate` returns the newest snapshot (404 before the first); `GET /api/rag/evaluate/{id}` returns one by id.
        * `POST /api/rag/evaluate?hybrid=true|false` passes that value as the `hybrid` field of every retrieval request (forcing keyword plus vector fusion on or off for the whole run without a restart) and records it as `properties.hybrid`; without the parameter every request carries null (each follows `rag.retrieval.hybrid-enabled`) and `properties.hybrid` is null.
        * `POST /api/rag/evaluate?rerank=true|false` does the same for the `rerank` field (null when absent, following `rag.retrieval.reranking-enabled`) and records `properties.rerank`; `?rerank=true` with no FilingReranker configured returns HTTP 400 before any question runs and stores no snapshot.
        * `POST /api/rag/evaluate?trace=true` (since 2026-09-13) stores each question's retrieval trace in the snapshot (Traces below) and records `properties.trace` true; absent or false stores none and records `properties.trace` false. Tracing changes no rank, metric, or miss.
        * `GET /api/rag/evaluate/{id}/evidence` (since 2026-09-13) returns the per-question evidence report of a stored snapshot as JSON; `?format=markdown` renders the same JSON as tables (Evidence report below). Read-only, no model call.
        * A snapshot carries `hitAt1`, `hitAt3`, `hitAt5`, `mrr`, `tickerHitAt5`, `window`, `retrievalStrategy`, the run `properties` (window, latestFilingsOnly, set, setCreatedOn, candidateCount, rerankingEnabled, hybridEnabled, keywordCandidateCount, rrfK, rrfVectorWeight, rrfKeywordWeight, rrfFigureWeight, hybrid, and since the reranker plumbing rerank, rerankCandidates, and reranker, the simple name of the reranker's user class or null when none is configured, and since the cross-encoder rerankerVersion, rerankedQuestions, and rerankFallbackQuestions, and since windowed scoring rerankerScoring (Cross-encoder reranker, Evaluation fields); snapshots stored earlier keep their properties as stored), per-question `results` (rank, matched chunk id, null on a miss, and the question's `retrievalStrategy`, null on a retrieval error or on earlier snapshots), `misses` with the top three returned chunks (chunk id, accession, section, similarity) or the retrieval error, and `slices` (`figure` and `nonFigure`, see Metrics). `slices` is stored inside the `results` JSONB document (no migration); snapshots stored before 2026-09-13 (id 75 and earlier, including 69) have no slices key and return `slices: null`; nothing is backfilled. Since the evaluation evidence work (2026-09-13) `properties` also carry `trace`, and a traced snapshot carries `traces` (Traces below), stored in the same document only when present; every snapshot stored earlier (ids up to 299) and every untraced one returns `traces: null`.
    * Traces (since 2026-09-13; plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md`, Milestone 1)
        * Why: snapshots kept only each question's rank and matched chunk, so whether a chunk was a reranker candidate, which chunks outranked an answer, and a chunk's reranked position when another chunk matched were not recorded. A trace records them for a run that asks for it.
        * Opt-in per run: `POST /api/rag/evaluate?trace=true` (`RetrievalEvaluationService.evaluate(hybrid, rerank, trace)`). Each question is then retrieved through `FilingRetrievalService.retrieveTraced`, which returns the response `retrieve` would for the same inputs, and the ranks and metrics are computed from that response exactly as without tracing. Nothing in a trace reaches `/api/rag/retrieve`, the recommendation tools, or a prompt, and tracing adds no model call: one embedding per question and, when reranking runs, one cross-encoder scoring call, as without tracing.
        * `traces`: one `{id, trace}` per question in set order; `trace` is null for a question whose retrieval threw (its error is in `results`). A `RetrievalTrace` records:
            * `vectorCandidates`, `keywordCandidates`, `figureCandidates`: each leg's size; keyword and figure are null when that leg did not run or failed.
            * `fused`: the fused, diversified candidate list in order (the diversified vector list when fusion did not run), each `{chunkId, fusedPosition, vectorRank, keywordRank, figureRank}`: position 1..n without gaps and the chunk's 1-based rank in each leg that returned it, null when absent or the leg did not run (a chunk repeated within a leg counts once, at its first position, the fusion rule). Chunks removed by diversification are not listed.
            * `rerank`: `outcome` `OFF` (reranking did not resolve on), `RERANKED`, or `FALLBACK` with `fallbackReason` either the reason class the service logs at WARN (`timeout`, `interrupted`, `failure`, `invalidEvidence`) or `noCandidates`, which is not logged (empty fused list, reranker not called) (Corrected 2026-09-13, Milestone 1 remediation 1, marker added in Milestone 2: the bullet as first written read "the reason class the service logs (`timeout`, `interrupted`, `failure`, `invalidEvidence`) or `noCandidates`", which let `noCandidates` read as a logged reason; the service logs only the four WARN classes and never logs `noCandidates`.); `inputCount`, the chunks given to the reranker (the first max(`rerank-candidates`, topK) of `fused`; null when OFF). When RERANKED, `candidates` lists every rerank input chunk once in reranked order as `{chunkId, fusedPosition, rerankedPosition, score, windowCount, windowScores}`; its first topK entries are the returned chunks. A FALLBACK records no candidates and no scores. `scoresNotRecorded` is null when every candidate carries its position and score; otherwise `reranker reports no scores` (the reranker's `rerankScored` returned no order, as the default does: only the returned chunks carry a position) or `scores inconsistent with the returned order` (CrossEncoderReranker takes its results from the head of its scored order, so it does not produce this; the response is unaffected).
            * `returnedChunkIds`: the response's results in order.
        * Scores are the cross-encoder's logits as float values (per-window logits in window order; the score is their maximum under `max-window`, the single entry under `head`), from the one scoring call that produced the order.
        * Storage: `traces` sits in the `results` JSONB document (no migration) and is written only when present, so an untraced snapshot's document has exactly the keys it had before (asserted by RetrievalEvaluationRepositoryTests, which also reads the committed snapshot 297 back unchanged with `traces` null). Size on the reproduction run below: 361,073 bytes of JSON text for the traced document, of which the `traces` value is 351,500; `pg_column_size` 71,276 bytes (the full breakdown and how each figure was measured: Cross-encoder reranker, Evaluation fields).
        * Tests (no live run): FilingRetrievalServiceTraceTests (identical responses traced and untraced for rerank off, on, timeout, reranker exception, and invalid evidence, and for the two rerankers that could break that before remediation 1; one reranker call per retrieval; fallback reason with no scores; fused positions, leg ranks, and rerank order; Cross-encoder reranker, Tests), TraceReproductionCheckTests (the reproduction check below, over the committed snapshot 297 with no database: a rebuilt run passes; one rank difference, a fallback with a rank difference, an error, all three together, and property mismatches each fail once with a message naming every item, compared as exact text), CrossEncoderRerankerTests (the scored method, C3), RetrievalEvaluationServiceTests (`trace=true` uses the traced path and stores one trace per question, an errored question's trace null; absent or false stores none with `properties.trace` false and equal results), RetrievalEvaluationRepositoryTests (round trip including float window scores, no `traces` key on an untraced document, the committed snapshot 297 and older rows read back with `traces` null), FilingRetrievalControllerTests (`?trace=` reaches the service).
        * Reproduction check (`RetrievalEvaluationTraceLiveTests`, opt-in `-Drag.evaluation.live=true`, model files under `models/`): enables the cross-encoder and snapshot 297's settings (rerank true, `rerank-candidates` 20, `max-window`, overlap 64, at most 4 windows, window 10, hybrid at its default, set v2) in its own Spring context only and compares the run with 297 through `TraceReproductionCheck`, which collects and prints every problem and then fails once naming, in order, every question whose rank or matched chunk differs from 297's, every question that fell back (strategy not reranked, with its trace's outcome and fallback reason), every errored question, and every run property that differs from 297's: all 20 of 297's recorded properties are compared, and the only property 297 lacks that the run may carry is `trace`, which must be true. After that it checks one RERANKED trace per question, the trace invariants on the live data, and that the stored traces read back equal. (Corrected 2026-09-13, Milestone 1 remediation 1: the test asserted the reranked and fallback counts before comparing questions, so a fallback that changed a rank failed naming no question, and it compared 17 listed settings plus `trace` and the two counts, leaving out `rerankingEnabled`, while this sentence said the recorded properties.) The snapshot is saved in a rolled-back transaction. Run: `set -a && source .env && set +a && ./mvnw -o test -Dtest=RetrievalEvaluationTraceLiveTests -Drag.evaluation.live=true` (add `-Drag.evaluation.trace-out=<file>` to write the stored row).
        * Verified 2026-09-13: exit 0; 42 questions, 0 differing from 297 in rank or matched chunk; hit@1 0.500000, hit@3 0.761905, hit@5 0.785714, MRR 0.633135 (297's values); `rerankedQuestions` 42, `rerankFallbackQuestions` 0; fused lists of 45 to 72 chunks, rerank input 20 for every question, 35 to 48 rows scored per question (median 43), and the rows per scoring call equal, in order, to the 42 evaluation calls logged for 297 (`latency-297.txt`) and to each question's summed `windowCount`; evaluation 60,092 ms after one warm-up retrieval; `retrieval_evaluations` 31 rows, max id 299, before and after (the rolled-back row took id 410 from the sequence). Evidence: `documentation/live-runs/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence/` (`run.log`; `live-test-trace-reproduction.log`; `traced-snapshot-297-settings.json`, row_to_json of the traced row before the rollback; `rows-before-after.txt`; `live-test-cross-encoder-scored.log`).
    * Evidence report (since 2026-09-13; plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md`, Milestone 2 with Amendment 1)
        * Why: a trace records what retrieval did, but not which stored chunks hold an accepted phrase or where the phrase sits in a chunk's tokens and the cross-encoder's rows. The report joins, per question, the snapshot's result, its trace, the bundled set's accepted phrases, and stored chunk text, and says of every value whether it was read, computed, or is not recorded, so a write-up can cite values instead of inferring them.
        * Endpoint: `GET /api/rag/evaluate/{id}/evidence` (RetrievalEvaluationController, so the integration token is required like every evaluation endpoint) returns JSON; `?format=json` is the same; `?format=markdown` returns `text/markdown;charset=UTF-8`, the JSON document rendered by EvidenceMarkdownRenderer, which reads nothing but that JSON. 404 for an unknown snapshot, 400 for any other format. Built by RetrievalEvidenceService: it reads the snapshot, the bundled set, and `sec_filing_chunks`, and tokenizes with the cross-encoder's tokenizer; it embeds nothing, scores nothing, calls no chat model, and writes nothing.
        * Basis: every value is `{value, basis, source | rule | reason}` (EvidenceValue). `observed`: read from the named source (snapshot `set_version`, `properties.*`, `results`, the trace, `sec_filing_chunks` at report time, the bundled set, the current configuration, or the loaded model files). `derived`: computed from observed values by the named rule. `unknown`: value null, with the reason. A null value under `observed` or `derived` is itself the finding, and its source or rule says what null means (a fused position of null is "not in the fused list"). The report has no `inferred` basis. Keys that name a row carry no basis: a question's id, ticker, kind, and text; a phrase's accession, section, and text; the chunk id heading a row.
        * Report level: `snapshotId`; `setVersion`; `set` (`properties.set`, or for a snapshot without it the bundled set whose version equals `set_version`, derived); `traced` (whether the snapshot stores traces); `settings`: `rerank`, `rerankCandidates`, `reranker`, `rerankerVersion`, `rerankerScoring` as recorded (unknown when the snapshot has no such property), `loadedModelVersion` (the loaded cross-encoder's version; unknown with reason `cross-encoder not loaded (no PassageTokenizer bean: rag.retrieval.cross-encoder.enabled is not true)` when it is not loaded, since Milestone 3 per plan amendment 3, before which the reason read `tokenizer unavailable`), `passageScoring`, `windowOverlapTokens`, `maxWindows` (parsed from `rerankerScoring`, derived; overlap and window count are null under head scoring), and `maxLength`, observed from the current configuration `rag.retrieval.cross-encoder.max-length` because snapshots do not record it.
        * Per question, in snapshot order: `rank`, `matchedChunkId`, `retrievalStrategy`, `error` (the snapshot's result); `rerankOutcome`, `fallbackReason`, `scoresNotRecorded`, `fusedCount`, `rerankInputCount` (the trace); `queryTokens`; `acceptedPhraseCount` and `phrases` (the bundled set); `ranking`, `bestAcceptedChunk`, `bestAcceptedPosition`, `rankedAbove`.
        * Per accepted phrase: `heldByStoredChunk` and `chunks`, every chunk of `sec_filing_chunks` with the phrase's accession and section whose text contains the phrase under RetrievalEvaluationService.matches (whitespace runs collapsed, trimmed, case-insensitive), whether or not retrieval returned it; false with no chunks when no stored chunk holds it.
        * Per holding chunk: `chunkTokens`, `windowLength` (W beside this question), `windowStarts` (the start token of each row under the recorded scoring), and for every occurrence of the phrase `characterSpan`, `tokenSpan`, `head` (`wholly`, `partly`, or `not`), and `windowsHoldingWholly` (1-based rows; empty when no row holds the whole span); from the trace `fusedPosition` (null: not in the fused list), `rerankInput` (true: a rerank input; false: not one; its source says both since Milestone 3, before which a false value carried the source text "null: not a rerank input"), `rerankedPosition`, `score`, `windowCount`, `windowScores`, and `returnedPosition` (null: not returned). Offsets are `[start, end)`. `windowStarts` and `windowsHoldingWholly` are arithmetic on the snapshot's recorded scoring, not a record of scoring: they are rows that were scored only for a chunk that is a rerank input of a RERANKED trace (`rerankInput` observed true); for reranking off, a fallback, a question without a trace, or a chunk outside the rerank input they are the rows the recorded scoring would score, not rows that were scored. Their rule text and the markdown legend say so. (Changed 2026-09-13, Milestone 3, plan amendment 2: before, `windowStarts` was described here and in its rule text as "the rows scored", which a write-up could cite as scored for a chunk the reranker never received.)
        * Ranking: `ranking` is the order the response was cut from, `reranked order` when the outcome is RERANKED and `fused order` when OFF or FALLBACK; `bestAcceptedChunk` and `bestAcceptedPosition` are the first chunk in it that holds any accepted phrase of the question (null when none is in it); `rankedAbove` lists every chunk ranked above that one with its position, fused position, score, and window scores, or the whole ranking when no holding chunk is in it. In fused order a score is observed null when reranking was off and unknown on a fallback.
        * Token position rules. A text is bounded as the scorer bounds it (CrossEncoderInputBounds, 20,000 characters) and tokenized alone by the PassageTokenizer bean: the verified `tokenizer.json` loaded through `OnnxCrossEncoderScorer.singleSequenceTokenizer`, the scorer's own builder, and encoded the scorer's way (no special tokens, no truncation, no padding); it exists only with `rag.retrieval.cross-encoder.enabled` true. The whole chunk is tokenized once with each token's character span (the native spans are Unicode code point offsets, converted to UTF-16 offsets). A phrase occurrence is found in the normalised chunk text and mapped back to stored-text offsets (a collapsed space maps to its whole whitespace run); its token span is every token of the whole-chunk tokenization whose character span overlaps it, so a word the phrase boundary cuts is counted and no prefix is tokenized separately. W = max-length - 3 - the query tokens kept against the whole chunk, longest first (CrossEncoderPairAssembler.windowLength); max-length comes from the current configuration, which snapshots do not record, and W's rule text says so (since Milestone 3, plan amendment 2). `head` is wholly when the span ends at or before W, partly when it starts before W and ends after it, not otherwise. `windowStarts` is one row at 0 under head scoring and CrossEncoderPairAssembler.windowStarts under max-window; row k holds tokens `[start, start + min(W, chunk tokens))`.
        * When a value is unknown. Candidate and score fields need the question's trace: reason `no trace` on a snapshot stored without traces; on a traced snapshot, for a question whose trace is null or absent, `no trace for this question (its retrieval failed)` only when the question's result records an error, otherwise `no trace recorded for this question` (Changed 2026-09-13, Milestone 3, plan amendment 2: before, every null trace was reported as a failed retrieval without reading the result's error); they are never filled from a default, another question, or another snapshot. On a FALLBACK the reranked positions, scores, and window scores are unknown (the trace records none) and `rerankInput` is derived from the fused position and `inputCount`; on RERANKED with `scoresNotRecorded` set, positions and scores the trace lacks are unknown with that reason. Token fields need the PassageTokenizer bean (`tokenizer unavailable`) and a snapshot `rerankerVersion` equal to the loaded model's version (`snapshot records no rerankerVersion`, or `snapshot rerankerVersion <recorded> differs from the loaded model version <loaded>`), so they are never computed with another model's tokenizer. `windowStarts` and `windowsHoldingWholly` also need a recorded, recognised `rerankerScoring` (`snapshot records no rerankerScoring`, `unrecognised rerankerScoring '<text>'`); W and `head` do not. Accepted phrases need the snapshot's set to load with the snapshot's version (otherwise `acceptedPhraseCount` is unknown with the reason and `phrases` is empty). A phrase position is unknown when the per-character normalisation cannot reproduce RetrievalEvaluationService.normalise (a context-dependent lower-casing such as a word-final capital sigma), or when the occurrence ends past the 20,000-character bound.
        * Limits: chunk text and accepted phrases are read at report time, so a filing rebuilt or a set edited after the snapshot is reported as it is now (chunk ids change on a rebuild); `maxLength` comes from the current configuration, not the snapshot; the report describes where a phrase sits and what the trace recorded, never why a chunk ranked where it did.
        * Tests (no live run): CrossEncoderTokenPositionsTests (D1: hand-computed token spans, head membership, and window indexes for a phrase straddling a window boundary, a phrase split by the head cut, overlap 0, 4, and 224, a chunk shorter than W, an empty chunk, the max-windows cap, head scoring, code point to UTF-16 conversion, and the scoring label round trip), PhraseOccurrencesTests (the normalisation mapping), RetrievalEvidenceServiceTests (scripted snapshot, set, chunks, and word tokenizer: D1 through the report, D2 every phrase against every holding chunk and a phrase held by none, D3 every candidate and score field unknown `no trace` on an untraced snapshot, D4 `tokenizer unavailable`, a different or missing model version, missing or unrecognised scoring, fallback, reranking off, a reranker without scores, set resolution, every value's basis well formed and no "inferred"; since Milestone 3 a null trace without a recorded error (`no trace recorded for this question`), a non-input chunk's `rerankInput` source, and the window and W rule text; the scripted report equals the committed `src/test/resources/evaluation/evidence/scripted-report.json`, and the untraced snapshot's report `scripted-report-untraced.json`), EvidenceMarkdownRendererTests (D5: that JSON renders exactly the committed `scripted-report.md`; the service's markdown is the rendering of its JSON; a value edited in the JSON alone changes the markdown; regenerate the three fixtures with `-Drag.evidence.fixture.write=true` on RetrievalEvidenceServiceTests after an intended change), RetrievalEvidenceEndpointTests (JSON, markdown equal to the rendering of the served JSON, 404, 400, and an answer without the tokenizer bean), RetrievalEvidenceDatabaseTests (the real store and default wiring, rolled back: on a filing and chunks the test inserts under an accession no real filing has, with the test set `evaluation/evidence/database-set.json`, a phrase is reported against exactly the inserted chunks holding it, a phrase no chunk holds is reported as held by none, trace fields `no trace`, token fields `tokenizer unavailable`, `loadedModelVersion` cross-encoder not loaded, so it holds on a freshly migrated database; every accepted phrase of the selected set against exactly the chunks SQL and the rule find in whatever is stored; and, opt-in with `-Drag.evaluation.live=true` since Milestone 3 per plan amendment 2, every accepted phrase of the selected set held by at least one stored chunk, which needs the filings ingested), IntegrationAccessTests (401 without the token), CrossEncoderConfigurationTests (no PassageTokenizer bean when disabled).
        * Live checks (opt-in): CrossEncoderPassageTokenizerLiveTests (`-Drag.rerank.live=true`, model files under `models/`): converted spans select each token's own characters on text with emoji, curly quotes, non-breaking spaces, and CJK, and the window arithmetic's row count equals the rows the real scorer ran for passages of 0 to 2,601 tokens under head and three max-window settings at max-length 512 and 64. RetrievalEvidenceLiveTests (`-Drag.evaluation.live=true`, cross-encoder enabled in its own context, reads snapshots 459 and 297 from the shared database, writes nothing to it; `-Drag.evidence.traced-snapshot` and `-Drag.evidence.untraced-snapshot` choose others; `-Drag.evidence.write-dir=<directory>` writes both reports there as compact JSON, `evidence-<id>.json`, the form committed as evidence): the arithmetic's row count equals the trace's `windowCount` for every rerank input, msft-05 lists chunks 460, 515, and 571 with derived token spans and chunk 515's rerank input membership observed, msft-04's chunk 466 has an observed reranked position, and snapshot 297's candidate fields are all unknown `no trace`.
        * Verified 2026-09-13: `./mvnw -q -o verify` exit 0; both live checks exit 0 (for snapshot 459 all 840 rerank inputs' recorded `windowCount` equal the arithmetic at the current max-length 512); on the running app with the cross-encoder enabled, `GET /api/rag/evaluate/459/evidence` answered 200 with 42 questions, 57 accepted phrases (none held by no stored chunk), 66 holding-chunk rows, 1,649 observed and 507 derived values and none unknown, and msft-05 listing chunks 460 (tokens [105, 121), wholly in the head, reranked 10), 515 (tokens [614, 625) of 700, not in the head, row 2; a rerank input, fused 14, reranked 12), and 571 (tokens [121, 132), wholly in the head, reranked 14); msft-04's chunk 466 reranked 11; `GET /api/rag/evaluate/297/evidence` gave the same phrases, chunks, and token fields with all 840 candidate and score values unknown `no trace`; 401 without the token, 404 for an unknown id, 400 for `format=html`; the fetched markdown equalled the renderer's output on the fetched JSON for both snapshots. Evidence: `documentation/live-runs/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence/` (`run.log`, section "Milestone 2"; `evidence-459.json` and `.md`; `evidence-297.json` and `.md`; `milestone-2-live-test-evidence.log`; `milestone-2-live-test-passage-tokenizer.log`). (Changed 2026-09-13, Milestone 3, plan amendment 2: committed evidence reports are compact JSON only, one line per file; the two `.md` files were removed, since the endpoint renders markdown from the JSON, and `evidence-459.json` and `evidence-297.json` were regenerated through RetrievalEvidenceLiveTests with `-Drag.evidence.write-dir` after the wording changes above. Parsed against the files fetched in Milestone 2 they differ only in rule and source text (four distinct strings in `evidence-459.json`, three in `evidence-297.json`): every value, basis, and other field is equal. Evidence: `run.log`, section "Milestone 3", and `milestone-3-evidence-json-comparison.txt`.)
    * Claims (since 2026-09-13; plan `plans/2026-09-13-evaluation-evidence.md`, Milestone 3 with Amendments 2 to 4, 6, and 7; Amendment 7 added to this heading 2026-09-14)
        * Why: the windowed reranker write-up failed validation on sentences the stored evidence could not support. A measurement's conclusions are written as claims in a file beside its evidence, each with a basis and a check the build evaluates against committed evidence, and the bullets of generated blocks in documentation are written from that file; prose outside those blocks is not generated or proven, may not cite a claim, and is read by people (What the check proves). An observed or derived sentence is rendered from its check, so it states the values that check found in the evidence; an inferred, unknown, or experiment sentence is free text that the checker screens for wording but does not prove (What the check proves). (Corrected 2026-09-13, remediation round 1, plan amendment 4: this bullet said that "a sentence cannot drift from its check", but the first version printed a hand-written `text` beside each check and never compared the two, so "Snapshot 297 ranks msft-05 first" passed with a check expecting rank 10 and "outside the top 5 in every row" passed with a check listing two rows.) (Corrected 2026-09-13, remediation round 2, plan amendment 6: this bullet said that documentation sections stating the conclusions are generated from the claims file without saying that only the bullets inside a block are, so a hand-written heading citing a claim above a block read as checked.)
        * Claims file: `documentation/live-runs/<date>-<feature>/claims.json`, a JSON object with `claims` (an array, in the order a generated block prints them), an optional `description` (a string), and optional `labels`. Each claim has `id` (`C-` and at least three digits, unique in the file; never renumber an id that documentation cites), `basis`, and `check`; `text` only on, and required on, `inferred`, `unknown`, and `experiment` claims (one line, no HTML comment markers); `from` only on `inferred` claims; `experiment` only on `experiment` claims; and an optional `block` (letters, digits, `_`, `-`) naming the block that prints it. `labels` maps a file a check reads (its path as the checks write it) to a name printed beside the id the file records, for example `"traced-snapshot-297-settings.json": "the traced reproduction run"`; a label is one line without digits or spelled-out numbers (numbers come from the evidence) and without a claim citation, passes the wording screen (Wording), and must name a file some check of the claims file reads; a file has at most one label, so two keys resolving to the same normalised path (`a.json` and `./a.json`) are a problem; the rule compares normalised paths, not the files they reach, so two spellings of one file that differ only in case on a case-insensitive file system, or a symbolic link inside the evidence root and its target, get two labels without a problem (corrected 2026-09-14: this sentence stated the rule with no limit). Free text may not contain a claim citation either. A key a format does not define is a problem at every level: the file, a claim, a check, a `topK` row, an `expected` object, a `ruleRow` `criteria` object, a claim's `experiment` object, an experiment file, and its `experiment` object. A key repeated within one JSON object is a read error. (Changed 2026-09-13, plan amendment 4: observed and derived claims no longer carry `text`; `labels` added; unknown keys were rejected only in the file, a claim, and a check, so a `topK` row with an extra `"rank": 7` passed.) (Changed 2026-09-13, remediation round 2, plan amendment 6: spelled-out numbers, citations, and a second label for one file were accepted; the second label was silently dropped.)
        * Bases. `observed` and `derived`: the claim's basis must be the basis of what its check reads. `rank` and `metric` read stored values (observed); `topK` and `ruleRow` compute from them (derived); `phraseSpan`, `membership`, and `candidate` take the basis of the report values they compare (derived if any compared value is derived, otherwise observed), and an `unknown` report value never matches. `inferred`: `from` lists the claims it follows from (at least one, each existing, not itself, no cycle) and it has no check; the checker does not evaluate the inference, but an inferred claim with no premise or with a premise that fails is a problem. `unknown`: must use `notRecorded`, and `notRecorded` is only for `unknown`. `experiment`: see Experiments.
        * Sentences (since 2026-09-13, plan amendment 4; every branch listed since remediation round 2, plan amendment 6). The generator prints `* <sentence> (C-nnn, <basis>)` at the start marker's indentation. An observed or derived sentence is rendered by its check type's template from the check's parameters and the values found in the evidence, never from the claim's expected values: when they differ the claim fails, and the sentence still states what the evidence holds. Below, `<Snapshot>` is `snapshot <id>` (the snapshot file's `id`) or, with a label, `<label> (snapshot <id>)`; `<Report>` is `the evidence report of snapshot <snapshotId>` or `<label> (the evidence report of snapshot <snapshotId>)`; a list `<a>, <b>, and <c>` is written `<a>` for one item, `<a> and <b>` for two, and with commas and a final `, and` for three or more; numbers from snapshots are printed to six decimal places. A claim whose sentence cannot be rendered (an unreadable or missing file, a malformed check, a snapshot without an `id`, a stored metric with more than six decimal places, free text that is not one line, an experiment failing its file rules, or an exception) has no sentence, and its block line is compared by citation only (The check). (Corrected 2026-09-13, remediation round 2, plan amendment 6: the templates below omitted the rank sentence without a recorded window, the membership row count of one or unknown, the unknown head and rows wordings, the reasons `rerank outcome <value>` and a rerank input that is not observed, the metric that is not recorded, the candidate field missing from the report and the per-field unknown wordings, a span that is not recorded, and how the experiment's checked sentence is changed; the trace's row count also read "1 scored rows".)
            * `rank`: "<Snapshot> ranks <question> <ordinal rank>." (for example "Snapshot 297 ranks msft-05 10th."); with no stored rank and no stored error, "<Snapshot> ranks <question> outside its window of <window> results (no matching chunk)." when the snapshot records its window and "<Snapshot> ranks <question> outside its window (no matching chunk)." when it does not; with a stored error, "<Snapshot> records a retrieval error for <question> and no rank." The first letter of `<Snapshot>` is capitalised ("Snapshot 297", "The traced reproduction run (snapshot 410)").
            * `topK`: "<question> is <group>." for one group, "<question> is <group>, and <group>." for two, and "<question> is <group>, <group>, and <group>." for three or more, where a group is "<inside|outside> the top <k> in <rows>", consecutive listed rows with the same outcome grouped in the listed order, and each row is `snapshot <id> (<detail>)` or `<label> (snapshot <id>, <detail>)` with detail `rank <n>` (also when n is above k), `no matching chunk in the window`, or `retrieval error, no rank`. The question id is printed as stored (not capitalised). The template has no "every" or "all": the sentence names exactly the rows the check lists, and a row listed twice fails the claim.
            * `metric`: "The <name> of <Snapshot> is <value>." When the snapshot does not record the metric, "The <name> of <Snapshot> is not recorded." (and the claim fails). The name is `hit@1`, `hit@3`, `hit@5`, `MRR`, `figure-slice <name>`, `non-figure-slice <name>`, or `<TICKER> hit@5`.
            * `ruleRow`: "Against <reference Snapshot>, <candidate Snapshot> <clauses>.", one clause per listed criterion in the listed order, joined as a list, each "<meets|does not meet> the <criterion> criterion (<values>)" with criterion `aggregate hit@5`, `non-figure hit@5`, `per-ticker hit@5`, or `FIGURE top-5`. Values: aggregate `hit@5 <candidate> against <reference>`; non-figure `non-figure-slice hit@5 <candidate> against <reference>`; either, when a value is absent, `<hit@5|non-figure-slice hit@5> not recorded (reference <value|none>, candidate <value|none>)`; per-ticker `the reference records no tickerHitAt5`, `at or above the reference's for <n> <ticker|tickers>: <TICKER> <candidate> against <reference>, ...`, or `below the reference's or not recorded: <item>, ..., of <n> <ticker|tickers>` listing only the failing tickers, each `<TICKER> <candidate> against <reference>` or `<TICKER> not recorded (reference <value|none>, candidate <value|none>)`; FIGURE top-5 `the reference records no kind for <id>, <id>`, `the reference has no FIGURE question in its top 5`, `the reference's FIGURE questions in its top 5 stay in the top 5: <id> (rank <n>, was <m>), ...`, or `left the top 5: <id> (<rank n|no rank>, was <m>), ...` listing only the questions that left. Lists inside values are joined with commas only.
            * `phraseSpan`: "In <Report>, occurrence <n> of <total> of <question>'s accepted phrase "<phrase>" in chunk <chunk> spans <spans>.", with only the spans the check lists, tokens first, joined as a list: `tokens [a, b)` or `tokens not recorded (<reason>)`, and `characters [c, d)` or `characters not recorded (<reason>)`. A reason is the report value's `reason`, `no reason given` when it has none, or `not in the report` when the report has no such value; a recorded value that is not a span is printed as stored.
            * `membership`: "In <Report>, occurrence <n> of <total> of <question>'s accepted phrase "<phrase>" in chunk <chunk> <head>, and <rows>." with both expected fields, "... in chunk <chunk> <head>." with `head` alone, and "... in chunk <chunk>: <rows>." with `windowsHoldingWholly` alone. `<head>` is `lies wholly inside the head window`, `lies partly inside the head window`, `lies outside the head window`, `has head membership <value>` for any other recorded value, or `has an unknown head membership (<reason>)`. `<rows>` is `the rows holding it wholly are unknown (<reason>)` when that value is unknown, otherwise "<which>, <held>": which is `of the <count> scored for this chunk` or `of the <count> the recorded scoring would score for this chunk (not rows that were scored: <why>)`, count is `1 row`, `<m> rows`, or `rows` when `windowStarts` is unknown; held is `no row holds it wholly`, `row <r> holds it wholly`, or `rows <r> and <s> hold it wholly` (a list for three or more). The words "scored for this chunk" are printed only when the question's `rerankOutcome` is observed `RERANKED`, the chunk's `rerankInput` is observed `true`, and its observed `windowCount` equals `<m>`, the length of `windowStarts` (the evidence report's rule for rows that were scored). Otherwise why is the first that applies: `rerank outcome unknown, <reason>` (`not in the report` without the value); `reranking was off`; `reranking fell back`; `rerank outcome <value>` for any other value; `rerank outcome RERANKED is <basis>, not observed`; `rerank input unknown, <reason>`; `rerank input <value>` when it is not a boolean; `the chunk was not a rerank input`; `rerank input true is <basis>, not observed`; `the trace records no window count, <reason>` (also for a recorded window count that is not an integer); `the trace records <n> <scored row|scored rows>, and the row count of this arithmetic is unknown` when `windowStarts` is unknown; `the trace records <n> <scored row|scored rows>, not the <m> of this arithmetic`.
            * `candidate`: "In <Report>, chunk <chunk>, which holds an accepted phrase of <question>, <parts>.", with only the fields the check lists, in the order fused position, rerank input, reranked position, joined as a list. A field the report's chunk lacks prints `has no <field> in the report` (the JSON name, for example `has no fusedPosition in the report`). `fusedPosition`: `was at fused position <n>`, `was not in the fused list` (null), or `has an unknown fused position (<reason>)`. `rerankInput`: `was a rerank input`, `was not a rerank input`, `has rerank input <value>` when it is not a boolean, or `has an unknown rerank input membership (<reason>)`. `rerankedPosition`: `was reranked <ordinal>`, `has no reranked position` (null), `has reranked position <value>` when it is not an integer, or `has an unknown reranked position (<reason>)`. A chunk holding several accepted phrases of the question is read where the report first lists it.
            * Free-text claims: `inferred` prints "<text> (inferred from <ids>)", the ids in the order `from` lists them, joined as a list; `unknown` prints "<text> (not recorded in <Report>: <reason>)", the reason read from the report (`no reason given` when it has none; once the value is recorded, and the claim fails, "<text> (recorded in <Report> as <value>, <basis>)"); `experiment` prints "<text> (experiment: <factor> <first setting> in snapshot <id> against <second setting> in snapshot <id>; checked: <checked>)", settings printed as stored (strings without quotes) and <checked> the check's rendered sentence without its final period and with its first letter lower-cased when the second letter is lower case ("Snapshot 296 ranks" becomes "snapshot 296 ranks" and "The hit@5 of" becomes "the hit@5 of"; "NVDA ..." and a sentence starting with a question id are unchanged).
        * Wording (since 2026-09-13, plan amendment 4; normalisation, lines introducing blocks, numbers, and the added words since remediation round 2, plan amendment 6; "mustn't", "'s behind", and "both" added to the limits 2026-09-14). The screen reads the free text of inferred, unknown, and experiment claims, every label, and the line introducing each generated block (Generated blocks). It reads each text three ways and names every distinct match: as written; normalised, with markdown emphasis and code marks (`*`, `_`, a backquote, `~`) removed; and normalised with those marks read as spaces. Normalising decodes numeric character references and the named ones `&shy;`, `&zwj;`, `&zwnj;`, `&ZeroWidthSpace;`, `&NoBreak;`, `&lrm;`, `&rlm;`, `&nbsp;`, `&amp;`, `&apos;`, `&rsquo;`, `&lsquo;`, `&ast;`, `&lowbar;`, `&grave;`; removes HTML tags and comments; keeps a markdown link's text and drops its target; removes every Unicode format character (category Cf: the soft hyphen U+00AD, the zero-width space, non-joiner, and joiner U+200B to U+200D, the word joiner U+2060, the byte order mark U+FEFF, and bidirectional marks); reads curly apostrophes as straight ones; and drops a backslash that escapes punctuation. A match found only after normalising is named with "(read with soft hyphens, invisible characters, and markdown and HTML marks removed)", so "caused" written with a soft hyphen inside it, `cau&shy;sed`, and `c**ause**d` are each refused as "caused". Stems are matched from the start of a word in any case; a stem written with a trailing hyphen here takes any ending, and a space matches any whitespace. Causal: caus-, because, result-, lead, leads, leading, led, driv-, drove, lift-, push-, boost-, improv-, trigger-, contribut-, stem from, stems from, stemmed from, stemming from, account for, accounts for, accounted for, accounting for, responsib-, reason-, why, thus, therefore, hence, consequen-, due to, thanks to, owing to, so that, attribut-, explain-, make, makes, made, making, affect-, effect-, impact-, help-, hurt-, enabl-, prevent-, forc-, induc-, yield-, rais-, put, puts, putting, giv-, gave, is behind, are behind, was behind, were behind, be behind, been behind, being behind, lie behind, lies behind, lay behind, source of, sources of, arise-, arising, arose, come from, comes from, came from, coming from, originat-, bring about, brings about, brought about, bringing about, on account of, in response to. Free text matching one needs basis `experiment`; a label or an introducing line matching one is refused. Absolute or predictive: always, never, will, won't, shall, shan't, any word followed by 'll (it'll), every-, all, none, nothing, "no" followed by whitespace or a hyphen and a word ("no row", "no trace", "no one", "no-one"), nobody, nowhere, guarantee-, invariabl-, forever, certain-, each, any-, whole, entire-, must, consistent-, going to, gonna, expect-, prove, proves, proved, proven, proving, proof; free text, a label, or an introducing line matching one is refused whatever the basis. The "no" rule refuses a determiner that generalises over every member of its noun and allows "not": an unknown claim writes "is not recorded", "does not record", or "not a rerank input", never "no trace". Numbers, in labels and introducing lines only: any run of Unicode number characters (digits, superscripts, fractions) and the spelled-out numbers zero to nineteen, twenty to ninety by tens (and fourty), hundred, thousand, million, billion, trillion, and dozen, each also with -s or -fold; the ordinals first to nineteenth, twentieth to ninetieth by tens, hundredth, thousandth, millionth, billionth, and trillionth, each also with -s; and once, twice, thrice, single, double, triple, quadruple, half, halves, quarter, quarters ("the forty-candidate row" is refused). Numbers in free text are allowed and not compared with anything. A claim citation, `(` and `C-` with digits, is refused in free text and labels. Every matching word is named. Limits: the screen matches words, not meaning. It refuses innocent uses (the noun "results", "the reason ... is not recorded", "all 42 questions", "the run made 42 calls", "each question", "the whole chunk", "the expected rank", "no row holds it", "the first row" in a label: reword, for example "whether ... is recorded", "the 42 questions", or "row 1 holds it" in free text), and it misses a cause or a generalisation worded otherwise ("rose once the overlap changed", "since", "after", "so", "typically", "in general", "usually", "most", "wholly", which is left unscreened because it names the report's membership value), a word split by a visible character ("cau-sed") or by markup other than the marks above, letters from another script that look like Latin ones, "mustn't", "'s behind", "both", numbers in free text that contradict the premises or the values checked, an inference drawn from an unknown claim, and experiment text naming a factor other than the one its file checks; those are read by people (What the check proves). (Replaces the Causes bullet of the first version, 2026-09-13, plan amendment 4: its whole-word list missed "resulting in", "leads NVDA to", "is why", "made it", "boosted", "the reason", and other inflections, and no absolute wording was screened. Changed 2026-09-13, remediation round 2, plan amendment 6: "raises", "put", "gives", "is behind", "is the source of", "each", "any", "whole", "entire", "no <noun>", "must", "consistently", "it'll", "going to", "expect", spelled-out numbers in labels, a soft hyphen inside a word, and markdown emphasis inside a word all passed, and lines introducing blocks were not screened.)
        * Experiments (since 2026-09-13, plan amendment 4). An `experiment` claim carries `experiment: {file, factor}`, a `text`, and a check of any type but `notRecorded` that verifies the stated outcome. The experiment file, resolved like any other file, is JSON with an optional `description` and `experiment: {factor, settings, snapshots}`: `factor` names a recorded property, `settings` lists two different values, and `snapshots` lists two committed snapshot files (relative to the experiment file), one per setting in the same order. The claim fails unless the file's factor equals the claim's; both snapshots can be read, record ids, and are different files; each setting equals the factor as its snapshot records it; the two recorded values differ; and every other recorded property is equal (the problem names each one that differs, with both values). Recorded properties are the snapshot columns `setVersion`, `questionCount`, and `windowSize`, and every key of `properties` except `trace`; ids, timestamps, metrics, results, traces, and the retrieval strategy are not compared. Every file the claim's check reads must be one of the two snapshots or an evidence report whose `snapshotId` is one of their ids.
